[s13.16] Psalm 25:3. 15-22. Third Part. Distress of soul; or, Backsliding and desertion.

[s13.17] 1 Mine eyes and my desire Are ever to the Lord; I love to plead his promises, And rest upon his word.

[s13.18] 2 Turn, turn thee to my soul, Bring thy salvation near: When will thy hand release my feet Out of the deadly snare?

[s13.19] 3 When shall the sovereign grace Of my forgiving God Restore me from those dangerous ways My wandering feet have trod?

[s13.20] 4 The tumult of my thoughts Doth but enlarge my woe; My spirit languishes, my heart Is desolate and low.

[s13.21] 5 With every morning light My sorrow new begins; Look on my anguish and my pain, And pardon all my sins.

## PAUSE.

[s14.1] 6 Behold the hosts of hell How cruel is their hate! Against my life they rise, and join Their fury with deceit.

[s14.2] 7 O keep my soul from death, Nor put my hope to shame, For I have plac'd my only trust In my Redeemer's name.

[s14.3] 8 With humble faith I wait To see thy face again; Of Israel it shall ne'er be said, "He sought the Lord in vain."

[s14.4] Psalm 26. Self-examination; or, Evidences of grace.

[s14.5] 1 Judge me, O Lord, and prove my ways, And try my reins, and try my heart; My faith upon thy promise stays, Nor from thy law my feet depart.

[s14.6] 2 I hate to walk, I hate to sit, With men of vanity and lies; The scoffer and the hypocrite Are the abhorrence of mine eyes.

[s14.7] 3 Amongst thy saints will I appear, With hands well wash'd in innocence; But when I stand before thy bar, The blood of Christ is my defence.

[s14.8] 4 I love thy habitation, Lord, The temple where thine honours dwell; There shall I hear thine holy word, And there thy works of wonder tell.

[s14.9] 5 Let not my soul be join'd at last With men of treachery and blood, Since I my days on earth have past Among the saints, and near my God.

[s14.10] Psalm 27:1. 1-6. First Part. The church is our delight and safety.

[s14.11] 1 The Lord of glory is my light, And my salvation too; God is my strength, nor will I fear What all my foes can do.

[s14.12] 2 One privilege my heart desires; O grant me an abode Among the churches of thy saints, The temples of my God!

[s14.13] 3 There shall I offer my requests, And see thy beauty still, Shall hear thy messages of love, And there enquire thy will.

[s14.14] 4 When troubles rise, and storms appear, There may his children hide: God has a strong pavilion where He makes my soul abide.

[s14.15] 5 Now shall my head be lifted high Above my foes around, And songs of joy and victory Within thy temple sound.

## Psalm 27:2. 8 9 13 14. Second Part.

[p27.1] Prayer and Hope.

[p27.2] 1 Soon as I heard my Father say, "Ye children, seek my grace;" My heart reply'd without delay, "I'll seek my Father's face."

[p27.3] 2 Let not thy face be hid from me, Nor frown my soul away; God of my life, I fly to thee In a distressing day.

[p27.4] 3 Should friends and kindred near and dear Leave me to want, or die, My God would make my life his care And all my need supply.

[p27.5] 4 My fainting flesh had dy'd with grief, Had not my soul believ'd To see thy grace provide relief, Nor was my hope deceiv'd.

[p27.6] 5 Wait on the Lord, ye trembling saints, And keep your courage up; He'll raise your spirit when it faints, And far exceed your hope.

[p27.7] Psalm 29. L. M. Storm and thunder.

[p27.8] 1 Give to the Lord, ye sons of fame, Give to the Lord renown and power, Ascribe due honours to his name, And his eternal might adore.

[p27.9] 2 The Lord proclaims his power aloud Over the ocean and the land; His voice divides the watery cloud, And lightnings blaze at his command.

[p27.10] 3 He speaks, and tempest, hail, and wind, Lay the wide forests bare around; The fearful hart, and frighted hind, Leap at the terror of the sound.

[p27.11] 4 To Lebanon he turns his voice, And, lo, the stately cedars break; The mountains tremble at the noise, The vallies roar, the deserts quake.

[p27.12] 5 The Lord sits sovereign on the flood, The thunderer reigns for ever king; But makes his church his blest abode, Where we his awful glories sing.

[p27.13] 6 In gentler language there the Lord The counsels of his grace imparts; Amidst the raging storm his word Speaks peace and courage to our hearts.

[p27.14] Psalm 30:1. First Part. Sickness healed, and sorrow removed.

[p27.15] 1 I will extol thee, Lord, on high, At thy command, diseases fly; Who but a God can speak and save From the dark borders of the grave?

[p27.16] 2 Sing to the Lord, ye saints of his, And tell how large his goodness is; Let all your powers rejoice and bless, While you record his holiness.

[p27.17] 3 His anger but a moment stays His love is life and length of days; Tho' grief and tears the night employ, The morning-star restores the joy.

[p27.18] Psalm 30:2. 6. Second Part. Health, sickness, and recovery.

[p27.19] 1 Firm was my health, my day was bright, And I presum'd 'twould ne'er be night; Fondly I said within my heart, "Pleasure and peace shall ne'er depart."

[p27.20] 2 But I forgot thine arm was strong, Which made my mountain stand so long; Soon as thy face began to hide, My health was gone, my comforts dy'd.

[p27.21] 3 I cry'd aloud to thee, my God, "What canst thou profit by my blood? "Deep in the dust can I declare "Thy truth, or sing thy goodness there?

[p27.22] 4 "Hear me, O God of grace," I said, "And bring me from among the dead:" Thy word rebuk'd the pains I felt, Thy pardoning love remov'd my guilt.

[p27.23] 5 My groans, and tears, and forms of woe, Are turn'd to joy and praises now; I throw my sackcloth on the ground, And ease and gladness gird me round.

[p27.24] 6 My tongue, the glory of my frame, Shall ne'er be silent of thy name Thy praise shall sound thro' earth and heaven, For sickness heal'd, and sins forgiven.

[p27.25] Psalm 31:1. 5 13-19 22 23. First Part. Deliverance from death.

[p27.26] 1 Into thine hand, 0 God of truth, My spirit I commit; Thou hast redeem'd my soul from death, And sav'd me from the pit.

[p27.27] 2 The passions of my hope and fear Maintain'd a doubtful strife, While sorrow, pain, and sin conspir'd To take away my life.

[p27.28] 3 "My times are in thine hand," I cry'd, "Tho' I draw near the dust ;" Thou art the refuge where I hide, The God in whom I trust.

[p27.29] 4 O make thy reconciled face Upon thy servant shine, And save me for thy mercy's sake, For I'm entirely thine.

## PAUSE.

[s16.1] 5 ['Twas in my haste, my spirit said, "I must despair and die, "I am cut off before thine eyes;" But thou hast heard me cry.]

[s16.2] 6 Thy goodness how divinely free! How wondrous is thy grace To those that fear thy majesty, And trust thy promises!

[s16.3] 7 O love the Lord, all ye his saints, And sing his praises loud; He'll bend his ear to your complaints, And recompense the proud.

[s16.4] Psalm 31:2. 7-13 18-21. Second Part. Deliverance from slander and reproach.

[s16.5] 1 My heart rejoices in thy name, My God, my help, my trust; Thou hast preserv'd my face from shame, Mine honour from the dust.

[s16.6] 2 "My life is spent with grief," I cry'd, "My years consum'd in groans, "My strength decays, mine eyes are dry'd, "And sorrow wastes my bones."

[s16.7] 3 Among mine enemies my name Was a mere proverb grown, While to my neighbours I became Forgotten and unknown.

[s16.8] 4 Slander and fear on every side, Seiz'd and beset me round; I to the throne of grace apply'd, And speedy rescue found.

## PAUSE.

[s17.1] 5 How great deliverance thou hast wrought Before the sons of men! The lying lips to silence brought, And made their boastings vain!

[s17.2] 6 Thy children, from the strife of tongues, Shall thy pavilion hide, Guard them from infamy and wrongs, And crush the sons of pride.

[s17.3] 7 Within thy secret presence, Lord, Let me for ever dwell; No fenced city, wall'd and barr'd, Secures a saint so well.

[s17.4] Psalm 32:1. S. M. Forgiveness of sins upon confession.

[s17.5] 1 O blessed souls are they Whose sins are cover'd o'er! Divinely blest, to whom the Lord imputes their guilt no more.

[s17.6] 2 They mourn their follies past, And keep their hearts with care; Their lips and lives without deceit, Shall prove their faith sincere.

[s17.7] 3 While I conceal'd my guilt I felt the festering wound, Till I confess'd my sins to thee, And ready pardon found.

[s17.8] 4 Let sinners learn to pray, Let saints keep near the throne; Our help in times of deep distress, Is found in God alone.

[s17.9] Psalm 32:2. First Part. L. M. Free pardon and sincere obedience; or, Confession and forgiveness.

[s17.10] 1 Happy the man to whom his God No more imputes his sin, But wash'd in the Redeemer's blood, Hath made his garments clean!

[s17.11] 2 Happy, beyond expression, he Whose debts are thus discharg'd; And from the guilty bondage free, He feels his soul enlarg'd.

[s17.12] 3 His spirit hates deceit and lies, His words are all sincere; He guards his heart, he guards his eyes, To keep his conscience clear.

[s17.13] 4 While I my inward guilt supprest, No quiet could I find; Thy wrath lay burning in my breast, And rack'd my tortur'd mind.

[s17.14] 5 Then I confess'd my troubled thoughts, My secret sins reveal'd; Thy pardoning grace forgave my faults, Thy grace my pardon seal'd.

[s17.15] 6 This shall invite thy saints to pray, When, like a raging flood, Temptations rise, our strength and stay Is a forgiving God.

[s17.16] Psalm 32:3. L. M. Repentance and free pardon; or, Justification and sanctification.

[s17.17] 1 Blest is the man, for ever blest, Whose guilt is pardon'd by his God, Whose sins with sorrow are confess'd, And cover'd with his Saviour's blood.

[s17.18] 2 Blest is the man to whom the Lord Imputes not his iniquities, He pleads no merit of reward, And not on works, but grace relies.

[s17.19] 3 From guile his heart and lips are free, His humble joy, his holy fear, With deep repentance well agree, And join to prove his faith sincere.

[s17.20] 4 How glorious is that righteousness That hides and cancels all his sins! While a bright evidence of grace Thro' his whole life appears and shines.

[s17.21] Psalm 32:4. Second Part. L. M. A guilty conscience eased by confession and pardon.

[s17.22] 1 While I keep silence, and conceal My heavy guilt within my heart, What torments doth my conscience feel! What agonies of inward smart!

[s17.23] 2 I spread my sins before the Lord, And all my secret faults confess; Thy gospel speaks a pard'ning word Thine Holy Spirit seals the grace.

[s17.24] 3 For this shall every humble soul Make swift addresses to thy seat; When floods of huge temptations roll, There shall they find a blest retreat.

[s17.25] 4 How safe beneath thy wings I lie, When days grow dark, and storms appear! And when I walk, thy watchful eye Shall guide me safe from every snare.

[s17.26] Psalm 33:1. First Part. C. M. Works of creation and providence.

[s17.27] 1 Rejoice, ye righteous, in the Lord, This work belongs to you: Sing of his name, his ways, his word, How holy, just, and true!

[s17.28] 2 His mercy and his righteousness Let heaven and earth proclaim; His works of nature and of grace Reveal his wondrous name.

[s17.29] 3 His wisdom and almighty word The heavenly arches spread; And by the Spirit of the Lord Their shining hosts were made.

[s17.30] 4 He bid the liquid waters flow To their appointed deep; The flowing seas their limits know, And their own station keep.

[s17.31] 5 Ye tenants of the spacious earth, With fear before him stand; He spake, and nature took its birth, And rests on his command.

[s17.32] 6 He scorns the angry nations' rage, And breaks their vain designs; His counsel stands thro' every age, And in full glory shines.

[s17.33] Psalm 33:2. Second Part. C. M. Creatures vain, and God all-sufficient.

[s17.34] 1 Blest is the nation where the Lord Hath fix'd his gracious throne; Where he reveals his heavenly word, And calls their tribes his own.

[s17.35] 2 His eye, with infinite survey, Does the whole world behold; He form'd us all of equal clay, And knows our feeble mould.

[s17.36] 3 Kings are not rescu'd by the force Of armies from the grave; Nor speed nor courage of an horse Can the bold rider save,

[s17.37] 4 Vain is the strength of beasts or men To hope for safety thence; But holy souls from God obtain A strong and sure defence.

[s17.38] 5 God is their fear, and God their trust, When plagues or famine spread, His watchful eye secures the just Amongst ten thousand dead.

[s17.39] 6 Lord, let our hearts in thee rejoice, And bless us from thy throne; For we have made thy word our choice, And trust thy grace alone.

[s17.40] Psalm 33:3. First Part. As the 113th Psalm. Works of creation and providence.

[s17.41] 1 Ye holy souls, in God rejoice, Your Maker's praise becomes your voice; Great is your theme, your songs be new: Sing of his name, his word, his ways, His works of nature and of grace, How wise and holy, just and true.

[s17.42] 2 Justice and truth he ever loves, And the whole earth his goodness proves, His word the heavenly arches spread; How wide they shine from north to south! And by the Spirit of his mouth Were all the starry armies made.

[s17.43] 3 He gathers the wide-flowing seas, Those watery treasures know their place, In the vast storehouse of the deep: He spake, and gave all nature birth; And fires, and seas, and heaven, and earth, His everlasting orders keep.

[s17.44] 4 Let mortals tremble and adore A God of such resistless power, Nor dare indulge their feeble rage: Vain are your thoughts, and weak your hands; But his eternal counsel stands, And rules the world from age to age.

[s17.45] Psalm 33:4. Second Part. As the 113th Psalm. Creatures vain, and God all-sufficient.

[s17.46] 1 O Happy nation, where the Lord Reveals the treasure of his word, And builds his church his earthly throne! His eye the heathen world surveys, He form'd their hearts, he knows their ways; But God their Maker is unknown.

[s17.47] 2 Let kings rely upon their host, And of his strength the champion boast; In vain they boast, in vain rely; In vain we trust the brutal force, Or speed, or courage of an horse, To guard his rider, or to fly.

[s17.48] 3 The eye of thy compassion, Lord, Doth more secure defence afford When death or dangers threatening stand; Thy watchful eye preserves the just, Who make thy name their fear and trust, When wars or famine waste the land.

[s17.49] 4 In sickness or the bloody field, Thou our physician, thou our shield, Send us salvation from thy throne; We wait to see thy goodness shine; Let us rejoice in help divine, For all our hope is God alone.

[s17.50] Psalm 34:1. First Part. L. M. God's care of the saints; or, Deliverance by prayer.

[s17.51] 1 Lord, I will bless thee all my days, Thy praise shall dwell upon my tongue; My soul shall glory in thy grace, While saints rejoice to hear the song.

[s17.52] 2 Come, magnify the Lord with me, Come, let us all exalt his name; I sought th' eternal God, and he Has not expos'd my hope to shame.

[s17.53] 3 I told him all my secret grief, My secret groaning reach'd his ears; He gave my inward pains relief, And calm'd the tumult of my fears.

[s17.54] 4 To him the poor lift up their eyes, Their faces feel the heavenly shine; A beam of mercy from the skies Fills them with light and joy divine.

[s17.55] 6 His holy angels pitch their tents Around the men that serve the Lord; O fear and love him, all his saints, Taste of his grace and trust his word.

[s17.56] 6 The wild young lions, pinch'd with pain And hunger, roar thro' all the wood; But none shall seek the Lord in vain, Nor want supplies of real good.

[s17.57] Psalm 34:2. 11-22. Second Part. L. M. Religious education; or, Instructions of piety.

[s17.58] 1 Children in years and knowledge young, Your parents' hope, your parents' joy, Attend the counsels of my tongue, Let pious thoughts your minds employ.

[s17.59] 2 If you desire a length of days, And peace to crown your mortal state, Restrain your feet from impious ways, Your lips from slander and deceit.

[s17.60] 3 The eyes of God regard his saints, His ears are open to their cries; He sets his frowning face against The sons of violence and lies.

[s17.61] 4 To humble souls and broken hearts God with his grace is ever nigh; Pardon and hope his love imparts When men in deep contrition lie.

[s17.62] 5 He tell their tears, he counts their groans, His Son redeems their souls from death; His Spirit heals their broken bones, They in his praise employ their breath.

[s17.63] Psalm 34:3. 1-10. First Part. C. M. Prayer and Praise for eminent deliverance.

[s17.64] 1 I'll bless the Lord from day to day; How good are all his ways! Ye humble souls that use to pray, Come, help my lips to praise.

[s17.65] 2 Sing to the honour of his name, How a poor sufferer cry'd, Nor was his hope expos'd to shame, Nor was his suit deny'd.

[s17.66] 3 When threatening sorrows round me stood, And endless fears arose, Like the loud billows of a flood, Redoubling all my woes;

[s17.67] 4 I told the Lord my sore distress With heavy groans and tears, He gave my sharpest torments ease, And silenc'd all my fears.

## PAUSE.

[s18.1] 5 [O sinners, come and taste his love, Come, learn his pleasant ways, And let your own experience prove The sweetness of his grace.

[s18.2] 6 He bids his angels pitch their tents Round where his children dwell What ills their heavenly care prevents No earthly tongue can tell.]

[s18.3] 7 [O love the Lord, ye saints of his; His eye regards the just; How richly blest their portion is Who make the Lord their trust!

[s18.4] 8 Young lions pinch'd with hunger roar, And famish in the wood; But God supplies his holy poor With every needful good.]

[s18.5] Psalm 34:4. 11-22. Second Part. C. M. Exhortations to peace and Holiness.

[s18.6] 1 Come, children, learn to fear the Lord; And that your days be long, Let not a false or spiteful word Be found upon your tongue.

[s18.7] 2 Depart from mischief, practise love, Pursue the works of peace; So shall the Lord your ways approve, And set your souls at ease.

[s18.8] 3 His eyes awake to guard the just, His ears attend their cry; When broken spirits dwell in dust, The God of grace is nigh.

[s18.9] 4 What tho' the sorrows here they taste Are sharp and tedious too, The Lord, who saves them all at last, Is their supporter now.

[s18.10] 5 Evil shall smite the wicked dead; But God secures his own, Prevents the mischief when they slide, Or heals the broken bone.

[s18.11] 6 When desolation like a flood, O'er the proud sinner rolls, Saints find a refuge in their God, For he redeem'd their souls.

[s18.12] Psalm 35:1. 1-9. First Part. Prayer and faith of persecuted saints; or, Imprecations mixed with charity.

[s18.13] 1 Now plead my cause, almighty God, With all the Sons of strife; And fight against the men of blood, Who fight against my life.

[s18.14] 2 Draw out thy spear and stop their way, Lift thine avenging rod; But to my soul in mercy say, "I am thy Saviour God."

[s18.15] 3 They plant their snares to catch my feet, And nets of mischief spread; Plunge the destroyers in the pit That their own hands have made.

[s18.16] 4 Let fogs and darkness hide their way, And slippery be their ground; Thy wrath shall make their lives a prey, And all their rage confound.

[s18.17] 5 They fly like chaff before the wind, Before thine angry breath; The angel of the Lord behind Pursues them down to death.

[s18.18] 6 They love the road that leads to hell; Then let the rebels die Whose malice is implacable Against the Lord on high.

[s18.19] 7 But if thou hast a chosen few Amongst that impious race, Divide them from the bloody crew By thy surprising grace.

[s18.20] 8 Then will I raise my tuneful voice To make thy wonders known; In their salvation I'll rejoice, And bless thee for my own.

[s18.21] Psalm 35:2. 12-14. Second Part. Love to enemies; or, The love of Christ to sinners typified in David.

[s18.22] 1 Behold the love, the generous love That holy David shows; Hark, how his sounding bowels move To his afflicted foes!

[s18.23] 2 When they are sick his soul complains, And seems to feel the smart; The spirit of the gospel reigns, And melts his pious heart.

[s18.24] 3 How did his flowing tears condole As for a brother dead! And fasting mortify'd his soul, While for their life he pray'd.

[s18.25] 4 They groan'd; and curs'd him on their bed, Yet still he pleads and mourns; And double blessings on his head The righteous God returns.

[s18.26] 5 O glorious type of heavenly grace! Thus Christ the Lord appears; While sinners curse, the Saviour prays, And pities them with tears.

[s18.27] 6 He, the true David, Israel's king, Blest and belov'd of God, To save us rebels dead in sin, Paid his own dearest blood.

[s18.28] Psalm 36:1. 5-9. L. M. The perfections and providence of God; or, General providence and special grace.

[s18.29] 1 High in the heavens, eternal God, Thy goodness in full glory shines; Thy truth shall break thro' every cloud That veils and darkens thy designs.

[s18.30] 2 For ever firm thy justice stands, As mountains their foundations keep; Wise are the wonders of thy hands; Thy judgments are a mighty deep.

[s18.31] 3 Thy providence is kind and large, Both man and beast thy bounty share; The whole creation is thy charge, But saints are thy peculiar care.

[s18.32] 4 My God! how excellent thy grace, Whence all our hope and comfort springs! The sons of Adam in distress Fly to the shadow of thy wings.

[s18.33] 5 From the provisions of thy house We shall be fed with sweet repast; There mercy like a river flows, And brings salvation to our taste.

[s18.34] 6 Life, like a fountain rich and free Springs from the presence of the Lord; And in thy light our souls shall see The glories promis'd in thy word.

[s18.35] Psalm 36:2. 1 2 5 6 7 9 C. M. Practical atheism exposed; or, The being and attributes of God asserted.

[s18.36] 1 While men grow bold in wicked ways! And yet a God they own, My heart within me often says, "Their thoughts believe there's none."

[s18.37] 2 Their thoughts and ways at once declare (Whate'er their lips profess) God hath no wrath for them to fear, Nor will they seek his grace.

[s18.38] 3 What strange self-flattery blinds their eyes! But there's an hastening hour When they shall see with sore surprise The terrors of thy power.

[s18.39] 4 Thy justice shall maintain its throne, Tho' mountains melt away; Thy judgments are a world unknown, A deep unfathom'd sea.

[s18.40] 5 Above the heavens' created rounds, Thy mercies, Lord, extend; Thy truth outlives the narrow bounds, Where time and nature end.

[s18.41] 6 Safety to man thy goodness brings, Nor overlooks the beast; Beneath the shadow of thy wings Thy children choose to rest.

[s18.42] 7 [From thee, when creature-streams run low, And mortal comforts die, Perpetual springs of life shall flow, And raise our pleasures high.

[s18.43] 8 Tho' all created light decay, And death close up our eyes Thy presence makes eternal day Where clouds can never rise.]

[s18.44] Psalm 36:3. 1-7. S. M. The wickedness of man, and the majesty of God; or. Practical atheism exposed.

[s18.45] 1 When man grows bold in sin My heart within me cries, "He hath no faith of God within, Nor fear before his eyes."

[s18.46] 2 [He walks awhile conceal'd In a self-flattering dream, Till his dark crimes at once reveal'd Expose his hateful name.]

[s18.47] 3 His heart is false and foul, His words are smooth and fair; Wisdom is banish'd from his soul, And leaves no goodness there.

[s18.48] 4 He plots upon his bed New mischiefs to fulfil; He sets his heart, and hand, and head, To practise all that's ill.

[s18.49] 5 But there's a dreadful God, Tho' men renounce his fear; His justice hid behind the cloud Shall one great day appear.

[s18.50] 6 His truth transcends the sky; In heaven his mercies dwell; Deep as the sea his judgments lie, His anger burns to hell.

[s18.51] 7 How excellent his love, Whence all our safety springs! O never let my soul remove From underneath his wings.

[s18.52] Psalm 37:1. 1-15. First Part. The cure of envy, fretfulness, and unbelief; or, The rewards of the righteous, and the wicked; or, The world's hatred, and the saint's patience.

[s18.53] 1 Why should I vex my soul and fret To see the wicked rise? Or envy sinners waxing great, By violence and lies.

[s18.54] 2 As flowery grass cut down at noon, Before the evening fades So shall their glories vanish soon In everlasting shades.

[s18.55] 3 Then let me make the Lord my trust, And practise all that's good; So shall I dwell among the just, And he'll provide me food.

[s18.56] 4 I to my God my ways commit, And cheerful wait his will; Thy hand, which guides my doubtful feet, Shall my desires fulfil.

[s18.57] 3 Mine innocence shalt thou display, And make thy judgments known, Fair as the light of dawning day, And glorious as the noon.

[s18.58] 6 The meek at last the earth possess, And are the heirs of heav'n; True riches with abundant peace, To humble souls are given.

## PAUSE.

[s19.1] 7 Rest in the Lord and keep his way, Nor let your anger rise, Tho' providence should long delay To punish haughty vice.

[s19.2] 8 Let sinners join to break your peace, And plot, and rage, and foam; The Lord derides them, for he sees Their day of vengeance come.

[s19.3] 9 They have drawn out the threatening sword, Have bent the murderous bow, To slay the men that fear the Lord, And bring the righteous low.

[s19.4] 10 My God shall break their bows, and burn Their persecuting darts, Shall their own swords against them turn, And pain surprise their hearts.

[s19.5] Psalm 37:2. 16 21 26-31. Second Part. Charity to the poor; or, Religion in words and deeds.

[s19.6] 1 Why do the wealthy wicked boast, And grow profanely bold? The meanest portion of the just Excels the sinner's gold.

[s19.7] 2 The wicked borrows of his friends, But ne'er designs to pay; The saint is merciful and lends, Nor turns the poor away.

[s19.8] 3 His alms with liberal heart he gives Amongst the sons of need; His memory to long ages lives, And blessed is his seed.

[s19.9] 4 His lips abhor to talk profane, To slander or defraud; His ready tongue declares to men What he has learn'd of God.

[s19.10] 5 The law and gospel of the Lord Deep in his heart abide; Led by the Spirit and the word, His feet shall never slide.

[s19.11] 6 When sinners fall, the righteous stand, Preserv'd from every snare; They shall possess the promis'd land, And dwell for ever there.

[s19.12] Psalm 37:3. 23-27. Third Part. The way and end of the righteous and the wicked.

[s19.13] 1 My God, the steps of pious men Are order'd by thy will; Tho' they should fall, they rise again, Thy hand supports them still.

[s19.14] 2 The Lord delights to see their ways, Their virtue he approves; He'll ne'er deprive them of his grace, Nor leave the men he loves.

[s19.15] 3 The heavenly heritage is theirs, Their portion and their home; He feeds them now, and makes them heirs Of blessings long to come.

[s19.16] 4 Wait on the Lord, ye sons of men, Nor fear when tyrants frown; Ye shall confess their pride was vain, When justice casts them down.

## PAUSE.

[s20.1] 5 The haughty sinner have I seen, Nor fearing man nor God, Like a tall bay-tree fair and green, Spreading his arms abroad.

[s20.2] 6 And lo! he vanish'd from the ground, Destroy'd by hands unseen: Nor root, nor branch, nor leaf was found Where all that pride had been.

[s20.3] 7 But mark the man of righteousness, His several steps attend; True pleasure runs thro' all his ways, And peaceful is his end.

[s20.4] Psalm 38. Guilt of conscience and relief; or, Repentance, and prayer for pardon and health.

[s20.5] 1 Amidst thy wrath remember love, Restore thy servant, Lord; Nor let a father's chastening prove Like an avenger's sword.

[s20.6] 2 Thine arrows stick within my heart, My flesh is sorely prest; Between the sorrow and the smart My spirit finds no rest.

[s20.7] 3 My sins a heavy load appear, And o'er my head are gone; Too heavy they for me to bear, Too hard for me t' atone.

[s20.8] 4 My thoughts are like a troubled sea, My head still bending down; And I go mourning all the day Beneath my Father's frown.

[s20.9] 5 Lord, I am weak, and broken sore, None of my powers are whole; The inward anguish makes me roar, The anguish of my soul.

[s20.10] 6 All my desire to thee is known, Thine eye counts every tear, And every sigh, and every groan Is notic'd by thine ear.

[s20.11] 7 Thou art my God, my only hope; My God will hear my cry; My God will bear my spirit up When Satan bids me die.

[s20.12] 8 [My foot is ever apt to slide, My foes rejoice to see't; They raise their pleasure and their pride When they supplant my feet.

[s20.13] 9 But I'll confess my guilt to thee, And grieve for all my sin, I'll mourn how weak my graces be, And beg support divine.

[s20.14] 10 My God, forgive my follies past, And be for ever nigh; O Lord of my salvation, haste, Before thy servant die.]

[s20.15] Psalm 39:1. 1 2 3. First Part. Watchfulness over the tongue; or, Prudence and zeal.

[s20.16] 1 Thus I resolv'd before the Lord, "Now will I watch my tongue, "Lest I let slip one sinful word, "Or do my neighbour wrong."

[s20.17] 2 And if I'm e'er constrain'd to stay With men of lives profane I'll set a double guard that day, Nor let my talk be vain.

[s20.18] 3 I'll scarce allow my lips to speak The pious thoughts I feel, Lest scoffers should th' occasion take To mock my holy zeal.

[s20.19] 4 Yet if some proper hour appear, I'll not be overaw'd, But let the scoffing sinners hear That I can speak for God.

[s20.20] Psalm 39:2. 4-7. Second Part. The vanity of man as mortal.

[s20.21] 1 Teach me the measure of my days, Thou maker of my frame; I would survey life's narrow space, And learn' how frail I am.

[s20.22] 2 A span is all that we can boast, An inch or two of time; Man is but vanity and dust In all his flower and prime.

[s20.23] 3 See the vain race of mortals move Like shadows o'er the plain; They rage and strive, desire and love, But all the noise is vain.

[s20.24] 4 Some walk in honour's gaudy show, Some dig for golden ore, They toil for heirs, they know not who, And straight are seen no more.

[s20.25] 5 What should I wish or wait for then From creatures, earth and dust? They make our expectations vain, And disappoint our trust.

[s20.26] 6 Now I forbid my carnal hope, My fond desires recall; I give my mortal interest up, And make my God my all.

[s20.27] Psalm 39:3. 9-13. Third Part. Sick-bed devotion; or, Pleading without repining.

[s20.28] 1 God of my life, look gently down, Behold the pains I feel; But I am dumb before thy throne, Nor dare dispute thy will.

[s20.29] 2 Diseases are thy servants, Lord, They come at thy command; I'll not attempt a murmuring word Against thy chastening hand.

[s20.30] 3 Yet I may plead with humble cries, Remove thy sharp rebukes; My strength consumes, my spirit dies Thro' thy repeated strokes.

[s20.31] 4 Crush'd as a moth beneath thy hand, We moulder to the dust; Our feeble powers can ne'er withstand, And all our beauty's lost.

[s20.32] 5 [This mortal life decays apace, How soon the bubble's broke! Adam and all his numerous race Are vanity and smoke.]

[s20.33] 6 I'm but a sojourner below, As all my fathers were, May I be well prepar'd to go When I the summons hear.

[s20.34] 7 But if my life be spar'd awhile, Before my last remove, Thy praise shall be my business still, And I'll declare thy love.

[s20.35] Psalm 40:1. 1 3 5 17. First Part. C. M. A song of deliverance from great distress.

[s20.36] 1 I waited patient for the Lord, He bow'd to hear my cry; He saw me resting on his word, And brought salvation nigh.

[s20.37] 2 He rais'd me from a horrid pit Where mourning long I lay, And from my bonds releas'd my feet, Deep bonds of miry clay.

[s20.38] 3 Firm on a rock he made me stand, And taught my cheerful tongue To praise the wonders of his hand, In a new thankful song.

[s20.39] 4 I'll spread his works of grace abroad; The saints with joy shall hear, And sinners learn to make my God Their only hope and fear.

[s20.40] 5 How many are thy thoughts of love! Thy mercies, Lord, how great! We have not words nor hours enough Their numbers to repeat.

[s20.41] 6 When I'm afflicted, poor and low, And light and peace depart, My God beholds my heavy woe, And bears me on his heart.

[s20.42] Psalm 40:2. 6-9. Second Part. C. M. The incarnation and sacrifice of Christ.

[s20.43] 1 Thus saith the Lord, "Your work is vain, "Give your burnt offerings o'er, "In dying goats and bullocks slain "My soul delights no more."

[s20.44] 2 Then spake the Saviour, "Lo, I'm here, "My God, to do thy will; "'Whate'er thy sacred books declare, "Thy servant shall fulfil.

[s20.45] 3 "Thy law is ever in my sight, "I keep it near my heart; "Mine ears are open'd with delight "To what thy lips impart."

[s20.46] 4 And see the bless'd Redeemer comes, Th' eternal Son appears, And at th' appointed time assumes The body God prepares.

[s20.47] 5 Much he reveal'd his Father's grace, And much his truth he shew'd, And preach'd the way of righteousness, Where great assemblies stood.

[s20.48] 6 His Father's honour touch'd his heart, He pity'd sinners' cries, And, to fulfil a Saviour's part, Was made a sacrifice,

## PAUSE.

[s21.1] 7 No blood of beasts on altars shed Could wash the conscience clean, But the rich sacrifice he paid Atones for all our sin.

[s21.2] 8 Then was the great salvation spread, And Satan's kingdom shook; Thus by the woman's promis'd seed The serpent's head was broke.

[s21.3] Psalm 40:3. 5-10. L. M. Christ our sacrifice.

[s21.4] 1 The wonders, Lord, thy love has wrought, Exceed our praise, surmount our thought; Should I attempt the long detail, My speech would faint, my numbers fail.

[s21.5] 2 No blood of beasts on altars spilt, Can cleanse the souls of men from guilt, But thou hast set before our eyes An all-sufficient sacrifice.

[s21.6] 3 Lo! thine eternal Son appears, To thy designs he bows his ears, Assumes a body, well prepar'd, And well performs a work so hard.

[s21.7] 4 "Behold, I come," (the Saviour cries, With love and duty in his eyes) "I come to bear the heavy load "Of sins, and do thy will, my God.

[s21.8] 5 "'Tis written in thy great decree, "'Tis in thy book foretold of me, "I must fulfil the Saviour's part, "And, lo! thy law is in my heart!

[s21.9] 6 "I'll magnify thy holy law, "And rebels to obedience draw, "'When on my cross I'm lifted high, "Or to my crown above the sky.

[s21.10] 7 "The Spirit shall descend, and show "What thou hast done, and what I do; "The wond'ring world shall learn thy grace, "Thy wisdom and thy righteousness."

[s21.11] Psalm 41. 1 2 3. Charity to the poor; or, Pity to the afflicted.

[s21.12] 1 Blest is the man whose bowels move, And melt with pity to the poor, Whose soul, by sympathising love, Feels what his fellow-saints endure.

[s21.13] 2 His heart contrives for their relief More good than his own hands can do; He, in the time of general grief, Shall find the Lord has bowels too.

[s21.14] 3 His soul shall live secure on earth, With secret blessings on his head, When drought, and pestilence, and dearth Around him multiply their dead.

[s21.15] 4 Or if he languish on his couch, God will pronounce his sins forgiv'n, Will save him with a healing touch, Or take his willing soul to heaven.

[s21.16] Psalm 42:1. 1-5. First Part. Desertion and hope; or, Complaint of absence from public worship.

[s21.17] 1 With earnest longings of the mind, My God, to thee I look; So pants the hunted hart to find And taste the cooling brook.

[s21.18] 2 When shall I see thy courts of grace, And meet my God again? So long an absence from thy face My heart endures with pain.

[s21.19] 3 Temptations vex my weary soul, And tears are my repast; The foe insults without control, "And where's your God at last?"

[s21.20] 4 'Tis with a mournful pleasure now I think on ancient days; Then to thy house did numbers go, And all our work was praise.

[s21.21] 5 But why, my soul, sunk down so far Beneath this heavy load? Why do my thoughts indulge despair, And sin against my God?

[s21.22] 6 Hope in the Lord, whose mighty hand Can all thy woes remove; For I shall yet before him stand, And sing restoring love.

[s21.23] Psalm 42:2. 6-11. Second Part. Melancholy thoughts reproved; or, Hope in afflictions.

[s21.24] 1 My spirit sinks within me, Lord, But I will call thy name to mind, And times of past distress record, When I have found my God was kind.

[s21.25] 2 Huge troubles, with tumultuous noise, Swell like a sea, and round me spread; Thy water-spouts drown all my joys, And rising waves roll o'er my head.

[s21.26] 3 Yet will the Lord command his love, When I address his throne by day, Nor in the night his grace remove; The night shall hear me sing and pray.

[s21.27] 4 I'll cast myself before his feet, And say "My God, my heavenly Rock, "Why doth thy love so long forget "The soul that groans beneath thy stroke?"

[s21.28] 5 I'll chide my heart that sinks so low, Why should my soul indulge her grief? Hope in the Lord, and praise him too, He is my rest, my sure relief.

[s21.29] 6 Thy light and truth shall guide me still, Thy word shall my best thoughts employ, And lead me to thine heavenly hill, My God, my most exceeding Joy.

[s21.30] Psalm 44. 1 2 3 8 15-26. The church's complaint in persecution.

[s21.31] 1 Lord, we have heard thy works of old, Thy works of power and grace, When to our ears our fathers told The wonders of their days:

[s21.32] 2 How thou didst build thy churches here, And make thy gospel known; Amongst them did thine arm appear, Thy light and glory shone.

[s21.33] 3 In God they boasted all the day, And in a cheerful throng Did thousands meet to praise and pray, And grace was all their song.

[s21.34] 4 But now our souls are seiz'd with shame, Confusion fills our face, To hear the enemy blaspheme, And fools reproach thy grace.

[s21.35] 5 Yet have we not forgot our God, Nor falsely dealt with heaven, Nor have our steps declin'd the road Of duty thou hast given.

[s21.36] 6 Tho' dragons all around us roar With their destructive breath, And thine own hand has bruis'd us sore Hard by the gates of death.

## PAUSE.

[s22.1] 7 We are expos'd all day to die As martyrs for thy cause, As sheep for slaughter bound we lie By sharp and bloody laws.

[s22.2] 8 Awake, arise, almighty Lord, Why sleeps thy wonted grace? Why should we look like men abhorr'd, Or banish'd from thy face?

[s22.3] 9 Wilt thou for ever cast us off And still neglect our cries? For ever hide thine heavenly love From our afflicted eyes?

[s22.4] 10 Down to the dust our soul is bow'd, And dies upon the ground; Rise for our help, rebuke the proud, And all their powers confound.

[s22.5] 11 Redeem us from perpetual shame, Our Saviour and our God; We plead the honours of thy Name, The merits of thy blood.

[s22.6] Psalm 45:1. S. M. The glory of Christ; the success of the gospel; and the Gentile church.

[s22.7] 1 My Saviour and my King, Thy beauties are divine; Thy lips with blessings overflow, And every grace is thine.

[s22.8] 2 Now make thy glory known, Gird on thy dreadful sword, And ride in majesty to spread The conquests of thy word.

[s22.9] Strike thro' thy stubborn foes, Or melt their hearts t'obey, While justice, meekness, grace, and truth, Attend thy glorious way.

[s22.10] 4 Thy laws, O God, are right; Thy throne shall ever end; And thy victorious gospel proves A sceptre in thy hand.

[s22.11] 5 [Thy Father and thy God Hath without measure shed His Spirit, like a joyful oil, T'anoint thy sacred head.]

[s22.12] 6 [Behold, at thy right hand The Gentile church is seen, Like a fair bride in rich attire, And princes guard the queen.]

[s22.13] 7 Fair bride, receive his love, Forget thy father's house; Forsake thy gods, thy idol gods, And pay thy Lord thy vows.

[s22.14] 8 O let thy God and King Thy sweetest thoughts employ; Thy children shall his honours sing In palaces of joy.

[s22.15] Psalm 45:2. C. M. The personal glories and government of Christ.

[s22.16] 1 I'll speak the honours of my King, His form divinely fair; None of the sons of mortal race May with the Lord compare.

[s22.17] 2 Sweet is thy speech and heavenly grace Upon thy lips is shed; Thy God, with blessings infinite, Hath crown'd thy sacred head.

[s22.18] 3 Gird on thy sword, victorious Prince, Ride with majestic sway; Thy terrors shall strike thro' thy foes, And make the world obey.

[s22.19] 4 Thy throne, O God, for ever stands; Thy word of grace shall prove A peaceful sceptre in thy hands, To rule the saints by love.

[s22.20] 5 Justice and truth attend thee still But mercy is thy choice; And God, thy God, thy soul shall fill With most peculiar joys.

[s22.21] Psalm 45:3. First Part. L. M. The glory of Christ, and power of his gospel.

[s22.22] 1 Now be my heart inspir'd to sing The glories of my Saviour-king, Jesus the Lord; how heavenly fair His form! how 'bright his beauties are!

[s22.23] 2 O'er all the sons of human race He shines with a superior grace, Love from his lips divinely flows, And blessings all his state compose.

[s22.24] 3 Dress thee in arms, most mighty Lord, Gird on the terror of thy sword, In majesty and glory ride With truth and meekness at thy side.

[s22.25] 4 Thine anger, like a pointed dart, Shall pierce the foes of stubborn heart; Or words of mercy kind and sweet Shall melt the rebels at thy feet.

[s22.26] 5 Thy throne, O God, for ever stands, Grace is the sceptre in thy hands; Thy laws and works are just and right, Justice and grace are thy delight.

[s22.27] 6 God, thine own God, has richly shed His oil of gladness on thy head, And with his sacred Spirit blest His first-born Son above the rest.

[s22.28] Psalm 45:4. Second Part. L. M. Christ and his church; or, The mystical marriage.

[s22.29] 1 The king of saints, how fair his face, Adorn'd with majesty and grace! He comes with blessings from above, And wins the nations to his love.

[s22.30] 2 At his right hand our eyes behold The queen array'd in purest gold; The world admires her heavenly dress, Her robe of joy and righteousness.

[s22.31] 3 He forms her beauties like his own; He calls and seats her near his throne: Fair stranger, let thine heart forget The idols of thy native state.

[s22.32] 4 So shall the King the more rejoice In thee, the favourite of his choice; Let him be lov'd and yet ador'd, For he's thy Maker and thy Lord.

[s22.33] 5 O happy hour, when thou shalt rise To his fair palace in the skies, And all thy Sons (a numerous train) Each like a prince in glory reign!

[s22.34] 6 Let endless honours crown his head; Let every age his praises spread; While we with cheerful songs approve The condescensions of his love.

[s22.35] Psalm 46:1. First Part. The church's safety and triumph among national desolations.

[s22.36] 1 God is the refuge of his saints, When storms of sharp distress invade; Ere we can offer our complaints Behold him present with his aid.

[s22.37] 2 Let mountains from their seats be hurl'd Down to the deep, and buried there; Convulsions shake the solid world, Our faith shall never yield to fear.

[s22.38] 3 Loud may the troubled ocean roar, In sacred peace our souls abide, While every nation, every shore, Trembles, and dreads the swelling tide.

[s22.39] 4 There is a stream whose gentle flow Supplies the city of our God; Life, love, and joy still gliding thro', And watering our divine abode.

[s22.40] 5 That sacred stream, thine holy word, That all our raging fear controls: Sweet peace thy promises afford, And give new strength to fainting souls.

[s22.41] 6 Sion enjoys her monarch's love, Secure against a threatening hour; Nor can her firm foundations move, Built on his truth, and arm'd with pow'r.

[s22.42] Psalm 46:2. Second Part. God fights for his church.

[s22.43] 1 Let Sion in her King rejoice, Tho' tyrants rage and kingdoms rise; He utters his almighty voice, The nations melt, the tumult dies.

[s22.44] 2 The Lord of old for Jacob fought, And Jacob's God is still our aid; Behold the works his hand has wrought, What desolations he has made!

[s22.45] 3 From sea to sea, thro' all the shores, He makes the noise of battle cease; When from on high his thunder roars, He awes the trembling world to peace.

[s22.46] 4 He breaks the bow, he cuts the spear, Chariots he burns with heavenly flame; Keep silence all the earth, and hear The sound and glory of his Name.

[s22.47] 5 "Be still, and learn that I am God, "I'll be exalted o'er the lands, "I will be known and fear'd abroad, "But still my throne in Sion stands."

[s22.48] 6 O Lord of hosts, almighty King, While we so near thy presence dwell, Our faith shall sit secure, and sing Defiance to the gates of hell.

[s22.49] Psalm 47. Christ ascending and reigning.

[s22.50] 1 O for a shout of sacred joy To God the sovereign King! Let every land their tongues employ, And hymns of triumph sing.

[s22.51] 2 Jesus our God ascends on high, His heavenly guards around Attend him rising thro' the sky, With trumpet's joyful sound.

[s22.52] 3 While angels shout and praise their King, Let mortals learn their strains; Let all the earth his honour sing; O'er all the earth he reigns.

[s22.53] 4 Rehearse his praise with awe profound, Let knowledge lead the song, Nor mock him with a solemn sound Upon a thoughtless tongue.

[s22.54] 5 In Israel stood his ancient throne, He lov'd that chosen race; But now he calls the world his own, And heathens taste his grace.

[s22.55] 6 The British islands are the Lord's, There Abraham's God is known, While powers and princes, shields and swords, Submit before his throne.

[s22.56] Psalm 48:1. 1-8. First Part. The church is the honour and safety of a nation.

[s22.57] 1 [Great is the Lord our God, And let his praise be great; He makes his churches his abode, His most delightful seat.

[s22.58] 2 These temples of his grace, How beautiful they stand! The honours of our native place, And bulwarks of our land.]

[s22.59] 3 In Sion God is known A refuge in distress; How bright has his salvation shone Thro' all her palaces!

[s22.60] 4 When kings against her join'd, And saw the Lord was there, In wild confusion of the mind They fled with hasty tear.

[s22.61] 5 When navies tall and proud Attempt to spoil our peace, He sends his tempests roaring loud, And sinks them in the seas.

[s22.62] 6 Oft have our fathers told, Our eyes have often seen, How well our God secures the fold Where his own sheep have been.

[s22.63] 7 In every new distress We'll to his house repair. We'll think upon his wondrous grace, And seek deliverance there.

[s22.64] Psalm 48:2. 10-14. Second Part. The beauty of the church; or, Gospel worship and order.

[s22.65] 1 Far as thy name is known The world declares thy praise; Thy saints, O Lord, before thy throne Their songs of honour raise.

[s22.66] 2 With joy let Judah stand On Sion's chosen hill, Proclaim the wonders of thy hand, And counsels of thy will.

[s22.67] 3 Let strangers walk around The city where we dwell, Compass and view thine holy ground, And mark the building well.

[s22.68] 4 The orders of thy house, The worship of thy court, The cheerful songs, the solemn vows; And make a fair report.

[s22.69] 5 How decent and how wise! How glorious to behold! Beyond the pomp that charms the eyes, And rites adorn'd with gold.

[s22.70] 6 The God we worship now Will guide us till we die, Will be our God while here below, And ours above the sky.

[s22.71] Psalm 49:1. 8-14. First Part. C. M. Pride and death; or, The vanity of life and riches.

[s22.72] 1 Why doth the man of riches grow To insolence and pride, To see his wealth and honours flow With every rising tide?

[s22.73] 2 [Why doth he treat the poor with scorn, Made of the self-same clay, And boast as tho' his flesh was born Of better dust than they?]

[s22.74] 3 Not all his treasures can procure His soul a short reprieve, Redeem from death one guilty hour, Or make his brother live.

[s22.75] 4 [Life is a blessing can't be sold, The ransom is too high; Justice will ne'er be brib'd with gold That man may never die.]

[s22.76] 5 He sees the brutish and the wise, The timorous and the brave, Quit their possessions, close their eyes, And hasten to the grave.

[s22.77] 6 Yet 'tis his inward thought and pride,-- "My house shall ever stand; "And that my name may long abide, "I'll give it to my land."

[s22.78] 7 Vain are his thoughts, his hopes are lost, How soon his memory dies! His name is written in the dust Where his own carcase lies.

## PAUSE.

[s23.1] 8 This is the folly of their way; And yet their sons, as vain, Approve the words their fathers say, And act their works again.

[s23.2] 9 Men void of wisdom and of grace, If honour raise them high. Live like the beast, a thoughtless race, And like the beast they die.

[s23.3] 10 Laid in the grave like silly sheep, Death feeds upon them there, Till the last trumpet break their sleep In terror and despair.

[s23.4] Psalm 49:2. 14 15. Second Part. C. M. Death and the resurrection.

[s23.5] 1 Ye sons of pride, that hate the just, And trample on the poor, When death has brought you down to dust, Your pomp shall rise no more,

[s23.6] 2 The last great day shall change the scene; When will that hour appear? When shall the just revive, and reign O'er all that scorn'd them here?

[s23.7] 3 God will my naked soul receive, When sep'rate from the flesh; And break the prison of the grave To raise my bones afresh.

[s23.8] 4 Heaven is my everlasting home, Th' inheritance is sure; Let men of pride their rage resume, But I'll repine no more.

[s23.9] Psalm 49:3. L. M. The rich sinner's death, and the saint's resurrection.

[s23.10] 1 Why do the proud insult the poor, And boast the large estates they have? How vain are riches to secure Their haughty owners from the grave!

[s23.11] 2 They can't redeem one hour from death, With all the wealth in which they trust; Nor give a dying brother breath, When God commands him down to dust.

[s23.12] 3 There the dark earth and dismal shade Shall clasp their naked bodies round; That flesh, so delicately fed, Lies cold, and moulders in the ground.

[s23.13] 4 Like thoughtless sheep the sinner dies, Laid in the grave for worms to eat; The saints shall in the morning rise, And find th' oppressor at their feet.

[s23.14] 5 His honours perish in the dust, And pomp and beauty, birth and blood: That glorious day exalts the just To full dominion o'er the proud.

[s23.15] 6 My Saviour shall my life restore, And raise me from my dark abode; My flesh and soul shall part no more, But dwell for ever near my God.

[s23.16] Psalm 50:1. 1-6. First Part. C. M. The last judgment; or, The saints rewarded.

[s23.17] 1 The Lord, the Judge, before his throne, Bids the whole earth draw nigh, The nations near the rising sun, And near the western sky.

[s23.18] 2 No more shall bold blasphemers say, "Judgment will ne'er begin," No more abuse his long delay To impudence and sin.

[s23.19] 3 Thron'd on a cloud our God shall come, Bright flames prepare his way, Thunder and darkness, fire and storm, Lead on the dreadful day.

[s23.20] 4 Heaven from above his call shall hear, Attending angels come, And earth and hell shall know and fear His justice and their doom.

[s23.21] 5 "But gather all my saints," he cries, "That made their peace with God, "By the Redeemer's sacrifice, "And seal'd it with his blood.

[s23.22] 6 "Their faith and works brought forth to light "Shall make the world confess "My sentence of reward is right, "And heaven adore my grace."

[s23.23] Psalm 50:2. 8 10 11 14 15 23. Second Part. C. M. Obedience is better than sacrifice.

[s23.24] 1 Thus saith the Lord, "the spacious fields "And flocks and herds are mine "O'er all the cattle of the hills "I claim a right divine.

[s23.25] 2 "I ask no sheep for sacrifice, "Nor bullocks burnt with fire; "To hope and love, to pray and praise, "Is all that I require.

[s23.26] 3 "Call upon me when trouble's near, "My hand shall set thee free; "Then shall thy thankful lips declare "The honour due to me.

[s23.27] 4 "The man that offers humble praise, "He glorifies me best; "And those that tread my holy ways "Shall my salvation taste."

[s23.28] Psalm 50:3. 1 5 8 16 21 22. 3d Part. C. M. The judgement of hypocrites.

[s23.29] 1 When Christ to judgment shall descend And saints surround their Lord, He calls the nations to attend, And hear his awful word.

[s23.30] 2 "Not for the want of bullocks slain "Will I the world reprove; "Altars and rites and forms are vain, "Without the fire of love.

[s23.31] 3 "And what have hypocrites to do "To bring their sacrifice? "They call my statutes just and true, "But deal in theft and lies.

[s23.32] 4 "Could you expect to 'scape my sight, "And sin without control? "But I shall bring your crimes to light, "With anguish in your soul."

[s23.33] 5 Consider, ye that slight the Lord, Before his wrath appear; If once you fall beneath his sword, There's no deliverer there.

[s23.34] Psalm 50:4. L. M. Hypocrisy exposed.

[s23.35] 1 The Lord, the Judge, his churches warns, Let hypocrites attend and fear, Who place their hope in rites and forms, But make not faith nor love their care.

[s23.36] 2 Vile wretches dare rehearse his name With lips of falsehood and deceit; A friend or brother they defame, And soothe and flatter those they hate.

[s23.37] 3 They watch to do their neighbours wrong; Yet dare to seek their Maker's face; They take his covenant on their tongue, But break his laws, abuse his grace.

[s23.38] 4 To heaven they lift their hands unclean, Defil'd with lust, defil'd with blood; By night they practise every sin, By day their mouths draw near to God.

[s23.39] 5 And while his judgments long delay, They grow secure and sin the more; They think he sleeps as well as they, And put far off the dreadful hour.

[s23.40] 6 O dreadful hour! when God draws near, And sets their crimes before their eyes! His wrath their guilty souls shall tear, And no deliverer dare to rise.

[s23.41] Psalm 50:5. To a new Tune. The last judgment.

[s23.42] 1 The Lord the Sovereign sends his summons forth, Calls the south nations, and awakes the north; From east to west the sounding orders spread Thro' distant worlds and regions of the dead: No more shall atheists mock his long delay; His vengeance sleeps no more: behold the day!

[s23.43] 2 Behold the Judge descends; his guards are nigh; Tempest and fire attend him down the sky: Heaven, earth and hell draw near; let all things come To hear his justice and the sinners doom: But gather first my saints (the Judge commands) Bring them, ye angels, from their distant lands.

[s23.44] 3 Behold! my covenant stands for ever good, Seal'd by the eternal sacrifice in blood, And sign'd with all their names; the Greek, the Jew, That paid the ancient worship or the new. There's no distinction here: come spread their thrones, And near me seat my favorites and my sons.

[s23.45] 4 I their almighty Saviour and their God, I am their Judge: ye heavens, proclaim abroad My just eternal sentence, and declare Those awful truths that sinners dread to hear: Sinners in Zion, tremble and retire; I doom the painted hypocrite to fire.

[s23.46] 5 Not for the want of goats or bullocks slain Do I condemn thee; bulls and goats are vain Without the flames of love: in vain the store Of brutal offerings that were mine before; Mine are the tamer beasts and savage breed, Flocks, herds, and fields, and forests where they feed.

[s23.47] 6 If I were hungry would I ask thee food? When did I thirst, or drink thy bullocks blood? Can I be flatter'd with thy cringing bows, Thy solemn chatterings and fantastic vows? Are my eyes charm'd thy vestments to behold, Glaring in gems, and gay in woven gold?

[s23.48] 7 Unthinking wretch! how couldst thou hope to please A God, a spirit, with such toys as these? While with my grace and statutes on thy tongue, Thou lov'st deceit, and dost thy brother wrong; In vain to pious forms thy zeal pretends, Thieves and adulterers are thy chosen friends.

[s23.49] 8 Silent I waited with lone-suffering love, But didst thou hope that I should ne'er reprove? And cherish such an impious thought within, That God the righteous would indulge thy sin? Behold my terrors now: my thunders roll, And thy own crimes affright thy guilty soul.

[s23.50] 9 Sinners, awake betimes; ye fools, be wise; Awake, before this dreadful morning rise; Change your vain thoughts, your crooked works amend, Fly to the Saviour, make the Judge your friend; Lest like a lion his last vengeance tear Your trembling souls, and no deliverer near.

[s23.51] Psalm 50:6. To the old proper Tune. The last judgment.

[s23.52] 1 The God of glory sends his summons forth, Calls the south nations, and awakes the north; From east to west the sov'reign orders spread, Thro' distant worlds, and regions of the dead: The trumpet sounds; hell trembles; heaven rejoices; Lift up your heads, ye saints, with cheerful voices.

[s23.53] 2 No more shall atheists mock his long delay; His vengeance sleeps no more; behold the day; Behold the Judge descends; his guards are nigh; Tempests and fire attend him down the sky. When God appears, all nature shall adore him; While sinners tremble, saints rejoice before him,

[s23.54] 3 "Heaven, earth, and hell, draw near; let all things come "To hear my justice and the sinner's doom; "But gather first my saints," the Judge commands, "Bring them, ye angels from their distant lands:" When Christ returns, wake every cheerful passion, And shout, ye saints; he comes for your salvation.

[s23.55] 4 "Behold my covenant stands for ever good, "Seal'd by th' eternal sacrifice in blood, "And sign'd with all their names, the Greek, the Jew, "That paid the ancient worship or the new." There's no distinction here: join all your voices, And raise your heads, ye saints, for heaven rejoices.

[s23.56] 5 "Here (saith the Lord) ye angels, spread their thrones: "And near me seat my favorites and my sons: "Come, my redeem'd, possess the joys prepar'd "Ere time began! 'tis your divine reward:" When Christ returns, wake every cheerful passion, And shout, ye saints; he comes for your salvation.

## PAUSE THE FIRST.

[s24.1] 6 "I am the Saviour, I th' almighty God, "I am the Judge: ye heavens, proclaim abroad "My just eternal sentence, and declare "Those awful truths that sinners dread to hear," When God appears all nature shall adore him; While sinners tremble, saints rejoice before him.

[s24.2] 7 "Stand forth, thou bold blasphemer and profane, "Now feel my wrath, nor call my threatenings vain, "Thou hypocrite, once drest in saint's attire, "I doom the painted hypocrite to fire." Judgment proceeds; hell trembles; heaven rejoices; Lift up your heads, ye saints, with cheerful voices.

[s24.3] 8 "Not for the want of goats or bullocks slain "Do I condemn thee; bulls and goats are vain "Without the flames of love; in vain the store "Of brutal offerings that were mine before:" Earth is the Lord's; all nature shall adore him; While sinners tremble, saints rejoice before him.

[s24.4] 9 "If I were hungry, would I ask thee food? "When did I thirst, or drink thy bullocks blood? "Mine are the tamer beasts and savage breed, "Flocks, herds, and fields, and forests where they feed:" All is the Lord's; he rules the wide creation: Gives sinners vengeance, and the saints salvation.

[s24.5] 10 "Can I be flatter'd with thy cringing bows, "Thy solemn chatterings and fantastic vows? "Are my eyes charm'd thy vestments to behold, "Glaring in gems, and gay in woven gold?" God is the judge of hearts; no fair disguises Can screen the guilty when his vengeance rises.

## PAUSE THE SECOND.

[s25.1] 11 "Unthinking wretch! how couldst thou hope to please "A God, a spirit with such toys as these! "While with my grace and statutes on thy tongue, "Thou lov'st deceit, and dost thy brother wrong!" Judgment proceeds; hell trembles; heaven rejoices: Lift up your heads, ye saints, with cheerful voices.

[s25.2] 12 "In vain to pious forms thy zeal pretends, "Thieves and adulterers are thy chosen friends; "While the false flatterer at my altar waits, "His harden'd soul divine instruction hates." God is the judge of hearts; no fair disguises Can screen the guilty when his vengeance rises.

[s25.3] 13 "Silent I waited with long suffering love; "But didst thou hope that I should ne'er reprove? "And cherish such an impious thought within, "That the All-Holy would indulge thy sin?" See, God appears; all nature joins t' adore him; Judgment proceeds, and sinners fall before him.

[s25.4] 14 "Behold my terrors now; my thunders roll, "And thy own crimes affright thy guilty soul; "Now like a lion shall my vengeance tear "Thy bleeding heart, and no deliverer near:" Judgment concludes; hell trembles; heaven rejoices; Lift up your heads, ye saints, with cheerful voices..

## EPIPHONEMA.

[s26.1] 15 Sinners, awake betimes; ye fools, be wise; Awake before this dreadful morning rise: Change your vain thoughts, your crooked works amend, Fly to the Saviour, make the Judge your friend: Then join the saints: wake every cheerful passion; When Christ returns, he comes for your salvation.

[s26.2] Psalm 51:1. First Part. L. M. A penitent pleading for pardon.

[s26.3] 1 Shew pity, Lord, O Lord, forgive, Let a repenting rebel live: Are not thy mercies large and free? May not a sinner trust in thee?

[s26.4] 2 My crimes are great, but not surpass The power and glory of thy grace; Great God, thy nature hath no bound, So let thy pardoning love be found.

[s26.5] 3 O wash my Soul from every sin, And make my guilty conscience clean; Here on my heart the burden lies, And past offences pain my eyes.

[s26.6] 4 My lips with shame my sins confess Against thy law, against thy grace: Lord, should thy judgment grow severe, I am condemn'd, but thou art clear.

[s26.7] 5 Should sudden vengeance seize my breath, I must pronounce thee just in death; And if my soul were sent to hell, Thy righteous law approves it well.

[s26.8] 6 Yet save a trembling sinner, Lord, Whose hope, still hovering round thy word, Would light on some sweet promise there, Some sure support against despair.

[s26.9] Psalms 51:2. Second Part. L. M. Original and actual sin confessed.

[s26.10] 1 Lord, I am vile, conceiv'd in sin; And born unholy and unclean; Sprung from the man whose guilty fall Corrupts the race, and taints us all.

[s26.11] 2 Soon as we draw our infant-breath, The seeds of sin grow up for death; Thy law demands a perfect heart, But we're defil'd in every part.

[s26.12] 3 [Great God, create my heart anew, And form my spirit pure and true: O make me wise betimes to spy My danger, and my remedy.]

[s26.13] 4 Behold I fall before thy face; My only refuge is thy grace: No outward forms can make me clean; The leprosy lies deep within.

[s26.14] 5 No bleeding bird, nor bleeding beast, Nor hyssop branch, nor sprinkling priest, Nor running brook, nor flood, nor sea, Can wash the dismal stain away.

[s26.15] 6 Jesus, my God, thy blood alone Hath power sufficient to atone; Thy blood can make me white as snow; No Jewish types could cleanse me so.

[s26.16] 7 While guilt disturbs and breaks my peace, Nor flesh, nor soul hath rest or ease; Lord, let me hear thy pardoning voice, And make my broken bones rejoice.

[s26.17] Psalm 51:3. Third Part. L. M. The backslider restored; or, Repentance and faith in the blood of Christ.

[s26.18] 1 O thou that hear'st when sinners cry, Tho' all my crimes before thee lie, Behold them not with angry look, But blot their memory from thy book.

[s26.19] 2 Create my nature pure within, And form my soul averse to sin; Let thy good Spirit ne'er depart, Nor hide thy presence from my heart.

[s26.20] 3 I cannot live without thy light, Cast out and banish'd from thy sight: Thine holy joys, my God, restore, And guard me that I fall no more.

[s26.21] 4 Tho' I have griev'd thy Spirit, Lord, His help and comfort still afford: And let a wretch come near thy throne To plead the merits of thy Son.

[s26.22] 5 A broken heart, my God, my King, Is all the sacrifice I bring; The God of grace will ne'er despise A broken heart for sacrifice.

[s26.23] 6 My soul lies humbled in the dust, And owns thy dreadful sentence just; Look down, O Lord, with pitying eye, And save the soul condemn'd to die.

[s26.24] 7 Then will I teach the world thy ways; Sinners shall learn thy sovereign grace; I'll lead them to my Saviour's blood, And they shall praise a pardoning God.

[s26.25] 8 O may thy love inspire my tongue! Salvation shall be all my song; And all my powers shall join to bless The Lord, my strength and righteousness.

[s26.26] Psalm 51:4. 3-13. First Part. C. M. Original and actual sin confessed and pardoned.

[s26.27] 1 Lord, I would spread my sore distress And guilt before thine eyes; Against thy laws, against thy grace, How high my crimes arise.

[s26.28] 2 Shouldst thou condemn my soul to hell, And crush my flesh to dust, Heaven would approve thy vengeance well, And earth must own it just.

[s26.29] 3 I from the stock of Adam came, Unholy and unclean; All my original is shame, And all my nature sin.

[s26.30] 4 Born in a world of guilt, I drew Contagion with my breath; And, as my days advanc'd, I grew A juster prey for death.

[s26.31] 5 Cleanse me, O Lord, and cheer my soul With thy forgiving love; O, make my broken spirit whole, And bid my pains remove.

[s26.32] 6 Let not thy Spirit quite depart, Nor drive me from thy face; Create anew my vicious heart, And fill it with thy grace.

[s26.33] 7 Then will I make thy mercy known Before the sons of men; Backsliders shall address thy throne, And turn to God again.

[s26.34] Psalm 51:5. 14-17. Second Part. C. M. Repentance and faith in the blood of Christ.

[s26.35] 1 O God of mercy! hear my call, My loads of guilt remove; Break down this separating wall That bars me from thy love.

[s26.36] 2 Give me the presence of thy grace, Then my rejoicing tongue Shall speak aloud thy righteousness, And make thy praise my song.

[s26.37] 3 No blood of goats, nor heifers slain, For sin could e'er atone; The death of Christ shall still remain Sufficient and alone.

[s26.38] 4 A soul opprest with sin's desert, My God will ne'er despise; A humble groan, a broken heart Is our best sacrifice.

[s26.39] Psalm 53. 4-6. Victory and deliverance from persecution.

[s26.40] 1 Are all the foes of Sion fools, Who thus devour her saints? Do they not know her Saviour rules, And pities her complaints?

[s26.41] 2 They shall be seiz'd with sad surprise; For God's revenging arm Scatters the bones of them that rise To do his children harm.

[s26.42] 3 In vain the sons of Satan boast Of armies in array: When God has first despis'd their host, They fall an easy prey.

[s26.43] 4 O for a word from Sion's King Her captives to restore! Jacob with all his tribes shall sing, And Judah weep no more.

[s26.44] Psalm 55:1. 1-8 16-18 22. C. M. Support for the afflicted and tempted soul.

[s26.45] 1 O God, my refuge, hear my cries, Behold my flowing tears, For earth and hell my hurt devise, And triumph in my fears.

[s26.46] 2 Their rage is levell'd at my life, My soul with guilt they load, And fill my thoughts with inward strife To shake my hope in God.

[s26.47] 3 With inward pain my heart-strings sound, I groan with every breath; Horror and fear beset me round Amongst the shades of death,

[s26.48] 4 O were I like a feather'd dove, And innocence had wings, I'd fly, and make a long remove, From all these restless things.

[s26.49] 5 Let me to some wild desert go, And find a peaceful home, Where storms of malice never blow, Temptations never come.

[s26.50] 6 Vain hopes, and vain inventions all To 'scape the rage of hell! The mighty God on whom I call Can save me here as well.

## PAUSE.

[s27.1] 7 By morning light I'll seek his face, At noon repeat my cry, The night shall hear me ask his grace, Nor will he long deny.

[s27.2] 8 God shall preserve my soul from fear, Or shield me when afraid; Ten thousand angels must appear If he command their aid.

[s27.3] 9 I cast my burdens on the Lord, The Lord sustains them all; My courage rests upon his word That saints shall never fall.

[s27.4] 10 My highest hopes shall not be vain, My lips shall spread his praise; While cruel and deceitful men Scarce live out half their days.

[s27.5] Psalm 55:2. 15-17 19 22. S. M. Dangerous prosperity; or, Daily devotions encouraged.

[s27.6] 1 Let sinners take their course, And choose the road to death; But in the worship of my God I'll spend my daily breath.

[s27.7] 2 My thoughts address his throne When morning brings the light; I seek his blessing every noon, And pay my vows at night.

[s27.8] 3 Thou wilt regard my cries, O my eternal God, While sinners perish in surprise Beneath thine angry rod.

[s27.9] 4 Because they dwell at ease, And no sad changes feel, They neither fear nor trust thy Name, Nor learn to do thy will.

[s27.10] 5 But I with all my cares Will call upon the Lord, I'll cast my burdens on his arm, And rest upon his word,

[s27.11] 6 His arm shall well sustain The children of his love; The ground on which their safety stands No earthly power can move.

[s27.12] Psalm 56. Deliverance from oppression and falsehood; or, God's care of his people, in answer to faith and prayer.

[s27.13] 1 Thou, whose justice reigns on high, And makes th' oppressor cease, Behold how envious sinners try To vex and break my peace!

[s27.14] 2 The Sons of violence and lies Join to devour me, Lord; But as my hourly dangers rise, My refuge is thy word.

[s27.15] 3 In God most holy, just, and true, I have repos'd my trust; Nor will I fear what flesh can do, The offspring of the dust.

[s27.16] 4 They wrest my words to mischief still, Charge me with unknown faults; Mischief doth all their councils fill, And malice all their thoughts.

[s27.17] 5 Shall they escape without thy frown? Must their devices stand? O cast the haughty sinner down, And let him know thy hand!

## PAUSE.

[s28.1] 6 God counts the sorrows of his saints, Their groans affect his ears; Thou hast a book for my complaints, A bottle for my tears.

[s28.2] 7 When to thy throne I raise my cry The wicked fear and flee; So swift is prayer to reach the sky, So near is God to me.

[s28.3] 8 In thee, most holy, just, and true, I have repos'd my trust; Nor will I fear what man can do, The offspring of the dust.

[s28.4] 9 Thy solemn vows are on me, Lord, Thou shalt receive my praise; I'll sing, "how faithful is thy word, "How righteous all thy ways!"

[s28.5] 10 Thou hast secur'd my soul from death; O set thy prisoner free, That heart and hand, and life and breath May be employ'd for thee.

[s28.6] Psalm 57. Praise for protection, grace, and truth.

[s28.7] 1 My God, in whom are all the springs Of boundless love and grace unknown, Hide me beneath thy spreading wings Till the dark cloud is overblown.

[s28.8] 2 Up to the heavens I send my cry, The Lord will my desires perform; He sends his angel from the sky, And saves me from the threatening storm.

[s28.9] 3 Be thou exalted, O my God, Above the heavens where angels dwell: Thy power on earth be known abroad, And land to land thy wonders tell.

[s28.10] 4 My heart is fix'd; my song shall raise Immortal honours to thy Name; Awake, my tongue, to sound his praise, My tongue, the glory of my frame.

[s28.11] 5 High o'er the earth his mercy reigns, And reaches to the utmost sky; His truth to endless years remains, When lower worlds dissolve and die.

[s28.12] 6 Be thou exalted, O my God, Above the heavens where angels dwell; Thy power on earth be known abroad, And land to land thy wonders tell.

[s28.13] Psalm 58. As the 113th Psalm. Warning to magistrates.

[s28.14] 1 Judges, who rule the world by laws, Will ye despise the righteous cause, When th' injur'd poor before you stands? Dare ye condemn the righteous poor, And let rich sinners 'scape secure, While gold and greatness bribe your hands?

[s28.15] 2 Have ye forgot, or never knew, That God will judge the judges too? High in the heavens his justice reigns? Yet you invade the rights of God, And send your bold decrees abroad, To bind the conscience in your chains.

[s28.16] 3 A poison'd arrow is your tongue, The arrow sharp, the poison strong, And death attends where'er it wounds: You hear no counsels, cries or tears; So the deaf adder stops her ears Against the power of charming sounds.

[s28.17] 4 Break out their teeth, eternal God, Those teeth of lions dy'd in blood; And crush the serpents in the dust: As empty chaff when whirlwinds rise, Before the sweeping tempest flies, So let their hopes and names be lost.

[s28.18] 5 Th' Almighty thunders from the sky, Their grandeur melts, their titles die, As hills of snow dissolve and run, Or snails that perish in their slime, Or births that come before their time, Vain births, that never see the sun.

[s28.19] 6 Thus shall the vengeance of the Lord Safety and joy to saints afford; And all that hear shall join and say, "Sure there's a God that rules on high, "A God that hears his children cry, "And will their sufferings well repay."

[s28.20] Psalm 60. 1-5 10-12. On a day of humiliation for disappointments in war.

[s28.21] 1 Lord, hast thou cast the nation off? Must we for ever mourn? Wilt thou indulge immortal wrath? Shall mercy ne'er return?

[s28.22] 2 The terror of one frown of thine Melts all our strength away; Like men that totter drunk with wine, We tremble in dismay.

[s28.23] 3 Great Britain shakes beneath thy stroke, And dreads thy threatening hand; O heal the island thou hast broke, Confirm the wavering land.

[s28.24] 4 Lift up a banner in the field, For those that fear thy Name; Save thy beloved with thy shield, And put our foes to shame.

[s28.25] 5 Go with our armies to the fight, Like a confederate God; In vain confederate powers unite Against thy lifted rod.

[s28.26] 6 Our troops shall gain a wide renown By thine assisting hand; 'Tis God that treads the mighty down, And makes the feeble stand.

[s28.27] Psalm 61. 1-6. Safety in God.

[s28.28] 1 When overwhelm'd with grief My heart within me dies, Helpless and far from all relief To heaven I lift mine eyes.

[s28.29] 2 O lead me to the rock That's high above my head, And make the covert of thy wings My shelter and my shade.

[s28.30] 3 Within thy presence, Lord, For ever I'll abide; Thou art the tower of my defence, The refuge where I hide.

[s28.31] 4 Thou givest me the lot Of those that fear thy Name; If endless life be their reward, I shall possess the same.

[s28.32] Psalm 62. 5-12. No trust in the creatures; or, Faith in divine grace and power.

[s28.33] 1 My spirit looks to God alone; My rock and refuge is his throne; In all my fears, in all my straits, My soul on his salvation waits.

[s28.34] 2 Trust him, ye saints, in all your ways, Pour out your hearts before his face: When helpers fail, and foes invade, God is our all-sufficient aid.

[s28.35] 3 False are the men of high degree, The baser sort are vanity; Laid in the balance both appear Light as a puff of empty air.

[s28.36] 4 Make not increasing gold your trust, Nor set your heart on glittering dust; Why will you grasp the fleeting smoke; And not believe what God hath spoke?

[s28.37] 5 Once has his awful voice declar'd, Once and again my ears have heard, "All power is his eternal due; "He must be fear'd and trusted too."

[s28.38] 6 For sovereign power reigns not alone, Grace is a partner of the throne: Thy grace and justice, mighty Lord, Shall well divide our last reward.

[s28.39] Psalm 63:1. 1 2 5 34. First Part. C. M. The morning of a Lord's day.

[s28.40] 1 Early, my God, without delay I haste to seek thy face; My thirsty spirit faints away, Without thy cheering grace.

[s28.41] 2 So pilgrims on the scorching sand, Beneath a burning sky, Long for a cooling stream at hand, And they must drink or die.

[s28.42] 3 I've seen thy glory and thy power Thro' all thy temple shine; My God, repeat that heavenly hour, That vision so divine.

[s28.43] 4 Not all the blessings of a feast Can please my soul so well, As when thy richer grace I taste, And in thy presence dwell.

[s28.44] 5 Not life itself with all her joys, Can my best passions move, Or raise so high my cheerful voice As thy forgiving love.

[s28.45] 6 Thus till my last expiring day I'll bless my God and King; Thus will I lift my hands to pray, And tune my lips to sing.

[s28.46] Psalm 63:2. 6-10. Second Part. Midnight thoughts recollected.

[s28.47] 1 'Twas in the watches of the night I thought upon thy power, I kept thy lovely face in sight Amidst the darkest hour.

[s28.48] 2 My flesh lay resting on my bed, My soul arose on high; "My God, my life, my hope," I said, "Bring thy salvation nigh."

[s28.49] 3 My spirit labours up thine hill, And climbs the heavenly road; But thy right hand upholds me still, While I pursue my God.

[s28.50] 4 Thy mercy stretches o'er my head The shadow of thy wings; My heart rejoices in thine aid, My tongue awakes and sings.

[s28.51] 5 But the destroyers of my peace Shall fret and rage in vain; The tempter shall for ever cease, And all my sins be slain.

[s28.52] 6 Thy sword shall give my foes to death, And send them down to dwell In the dark caverns of the earth, Or to the deeps of hell.

[s28.53] Psalm 63:3. L. M. Longing after God; or, The love of God better than life.

[s28.54] 1 Great God, indulge my humble claim Thou art my hope, my joy, my rest; The glories that compose thy Name Stand all engag'd to make me blest.

[s28.55] 2 Thou great and good, thou just and wise, Thou art my Father and my God; And I am thine by sacred ties; Thy son, thy servant bought with blood.

[s28.56] 3 With heart, and eyes and lifted hands, For thee I long, to thee I look, As travellers in thirsty lands Pant for the cooling water-brook.

[s28.57] 4 With early feet I love t' appear Among thy saints and seek thy face; Oft have I seen thy glory there, And felt the power of sovereign grace.

[s28.58] 5 Not fruits nor wines that tempt our taste, Not all the joys our senses know, Could make me so divinely blest Or raise my cheerful passions so.

[s28.59] 6 My life itself without thy love No taste of pleasure could afford; 'Twould but a tiresome burden prove, If I were banish'd from the Lord.

[s28.60] 7 Amidst the wakeful hours of night, When busy cares afflict my head One thought of thee gives new delight, And adds refreshment to my bed.

[s28.61] 8 I'll lift my hands, I'll raise my voice, While I have breath to pray or praise; This work shall make my heart rejoice, And spend the remnant of my days.

[s28.62] Psalm 63:4. S. M. Seeking God.

[s28.63] 1 My God, permit my tongue This joy, to call thee mine, And let my early cries prevail To taste thy love divine.

[s28.64] 2 My thirsty fainting soul Thy mercy doth implore; Not travellers in desert lands Can pant for water more.

[s28.65] 3 Within thy churches, Lord, I long to find my place, Thy power and glory to behold, And feel thy quickening grace.

[s28.66] 4 For life without thy love No relish can afford; No joy can be compar'd to this, To serve and please the Lord.

[s28.67] 5 To thee I'll lift my hands, And praise thee while I live; Not the rich dainties of a feast Such food or pleasure give.

[s28.68] 6 In wakeful hours at night I call my God to mind; I think how wise thy counsels are, And all thy dealings kind.

[s28.69] 7 Since thou hast been my help, To thee my spirit flies, And on thy watchful providence My cheerful hope relies.

[s28.70] 8 The shadow of thy wings My soul in safety keeps; I follow where my Father leads, And he supports my steps.

[s28.71] Psalm 65:1. 1-5. First Part. L. M. Public prayer and praise.

[s28.72] 1 The praise of Sion waits for thee, My God; and praise becomes thy house; There shall thy saints thy glory see, And there perform their public vows.

[s28.73] 2 O thou, whose mercy bends the skies To save when humble sinners pray, All lands to thee shall lift their eyes And islands of the northern sea.
